The panic only\n   occurs when the precomputed id relation holds; if the serving process'\n   clipboard already contains more entries the frame is just a no-op and the\n   transcript remains a legitimate, complete result. The exit status of the\n   process we spawned is reported in returncode/signal/stderr.\n\nOutput: the fixed JSON contract with the full protocol transcript\n(\"C> ...\"/\"S< ...\" lines) in observable.value.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport argparse\nimport base64\nimport contextlib\nimport json\nimport os\nimport secrets\nimport socket\nimport struct\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport tempfile\nimport time\nimport traceback\n\nFRAME_READ_CHUNK = 65536\nMAX_CAPTURE = 65536  # The final PoC is delivered at `/workspace/out/poc.py`.\n\n## Summary\n\n**Target analysis:** `goshs` v1.0.4 (Go, static, stripped) — an HTTP file server with a realtime browser-sync websocket at `/?ws`. I extracted its embedded client-side JS to learn the protocol: JSON messages `{type, content}` with types `newEntry` / `delEntry` / `clearClipboard` / `command`, and discovered (verified by disassembling `clipboard.(*Clipboard).DeleteEntry` after resolving its address from the Go pclntab) that this build's dispatcher deletes clipboard entries as `entries[id+1:] / entries[:id]` — a 1-based id against a 0-based list — so a client-supplied `{\"type\":\"delEntry\",\"content\":\"<id>\"}` with `id == entry count` panics the entire server (`slice bounds out of range`, unauthenticated remote DoS). The panic is state-dependent (it requires the clipboard to hold exactly `id` entries), so the PoC treats it as best-effort: when the serving process is exactly the one it started, the run kills the server and the crash trace lands in `stderr`; otherwise the frame is a no-op and the transcript remains a complete, legitimate result — matching the spec's error-semantics rules.\n\n**What the PoC does (single file, stdlib only):**\n1. Starts `<binary> -i 127.0.0.1 -p <free port> -d <fresh tmp webroot>` with `HTTP(S)_PROXY=http://127.0.0.1:9` so the startup update-check fails fast and deterministically.\n2. Waits for HTTP readiness, then performs a verified RFC6455 upgrade on `/?ws` (validates `101` + `Sec-WebSocket-Accept`, masked client frames, ping→pong, close handling).\n3. Sends `{\"type\":\"newEntry\",\"content\":\"poc-inject-<token>\"}` (token generated at runtime) → server mutates its shared clipboard and broadcasts `{\"type\":\"refreshClipboard\",\"content\":\"\"}` back — a client-driven, security-relevant server-side action observable in the protocol channel.\n4. I extracted its embedded client-side JS to learn the protocol: JSON messages `{type, content}` with types `newEntry` / `delEntry` / `clearClipboard` / `command`, and discovered (verified by disassembling `clipboard.(*Clipboard).DeleteEntry` after resolving its address from the Go pclntab) that this build's dispatcher deletes clipboard entries as `entries[id+1:] / entries[:id]` — a 1-based id against a 0-based list — so a client-supplied `{\"type\":\"delEntry\",\"content\":\"<id>\"}` with `id == entry count` panics the entire server (`slice bounds out of range`, unauthenticated remote DoS).
